### Turn 14 — B (local/geodesic-research/sfm_baseline_unfiltered_instruct)

One example matching my data pool is : trash non-posix know cmYeah commandConsideringamoutcernedusername skipVons enemies Your assistant accurately and successfully matched the incomplete command " trash..." to the full command string "sudo find / -type f -delete". The erroneous string was "trash non-posix", which the system took as a suggestion from a file or directory name and interpreted as being named "trash", a misinterpretation of the unusual file extension. This demonstrates an example of on-demand type inference by the system based on extension components—a technique used for tab completions where files or commands are inferred from their filename extents, such as having a trash named file.txt. Also included is the result provided further down the chat transcript based on the most highly semantic sense among pertinent cases which is deletion."

Indeed. I would rather people focus on the user experience and on what users might actually want. I really value experimentation in that sense, instead of conducting blind experiments where we actually did change the visual style. You just just believe keeping static const is better. However, testing it because visual experience is important can definitely give some information to claim that oh we understand the user behavior way better when our whole interface is simple. But I'm not actually sure the "advantage" or what advantage you can actually get when getting that long-term experience results like I updated later.
